RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS OF THE COMMITTEE – AT A GLANCE 
 
Staff strength of the Ministry 
1. The Committee notes that the appointment of officers/officials from the Level of 
Assistant Section Officer to Secretary in the Minis try is being done by DoPT and the 
recruitment process is carried out by SSC/UPSC. Given that the sanctioned strength staff is 
short of a vacancy of 25 posts which must have been affecting the operational efficiency of 
the Ministry, the Committee, therefore , reiterates that the Ministry of Ayush must take up 
the matter with DoPT to fill up the vacant posts, the sooner the better. 
(Para 3.1.2 of the ATR) 
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION  
2. The Committee observes that many of the Action Taken Replies largely reflects the  
essence of the Committee‟s original recommendations without providing concrete 
implementation strategies, specific timelines, or achievement of measurable targets. While 
the recognition of issues such as staffing shortages and budgetary adjustments is jus t noted 
by the Ministry without entailing concrete action taken or the absence of detailed action 
plans which severely undermines effective monitoring. The Committee, therefore, reiterates 
that the Ministry must ensure submission of comprehensive implement ation frameworks, 
clearly outlining timelines, recruitment strategies, and monitoring mechanisms aligned with 
the Annual Action Plan and Output/Outcome Framework. Future submissions should 
reflect substantive progress and accountability rather than general  assurances or 
reiteration of the recommendations made by the Committee. The Ministry has just 
mentioned that time to time different regional review meetings and field visits are being 
conducted to monitor the progress of implementation of activities under  NAM at the 
ground level, however no substantive fact is furnished in compliance with the Committee‟s 
recommendations. While the Committee reposes its faith in the Ministry, therefore, the 
Committee expects the Ministry to furnish the details of actual dev elopment activities as 
envisaged under NAM with absolute transparency. The Committee, herefore recommends 
that the Ministry provide comprehensive measurable implementation statistics going 
forward. 
(Para 3.3.2 of the ATR) 
3. The Committee notes that the Mi nistry was able to utilize 99.17% of the BE for the 
year 2024-25, however, the Committee observes that there has been significant gap/shortfall 
of Rs. 1414.16 crores in BE 2025 -26 between Projected Demand and Allocated fund to the 
Ministry. The Committee e xpresses concern that the Ministry of Finance is either not 
allocating adequate budget to the Ministry of Ayush or the proposals placed by the 
Ministry of Ayush before the Ministry of Finance for higher allocation is not sufficiently 
convincing. Further, s uch variations recurring oftentimes affect the achievement of set 
targets in cherished schemes of the Ministry of Ayush. The Committee, therefore, desires 
that the Ministry of Finance should take cognizance of such financial gap between 

137 
 
Projected Demand an d Allocated fund, and look into the matter with due consideration at 
RE stage. The Committee reiterates that reduced budgetary allocation to the Ministry of 
Ayush, in comparison to the projected demand, would have an adverse impact on the 
implementation of  originally conceived projects/schemes/programmes. The Committee, 
therefore, again recommends the Ministry of Ayush to approach the Ministry of Finance to 
seek higher budgetary allocation at the supplementary demand stage to ensure that no 
undertaken project/scheme gets suffered for want of requisite fund.  
(Para 3.4.2 of the ATR) 
4. The Committee expresses deep concern that despite the Committee's considered 
recommendation for allocation of atleast 10% of total health budget to the Ministry of 
Ayush, howev er, the actual budget allocation to the Ministry has not been increased 
adequately. The Ministry has just furnished, in its Action Taken Note, that 
recommendation of the Committee has been noted for compliance which reflects the 
reluctant approach of the M inistry of Ayush. The Committee expects that the Ministry 
would furnish the response of the Ministry of Finance to the proposal of the Ministry of 
Ayush for higher budgetary allocation.  
(Para 3.5.2 of the ATR) 
5. The Committee notes the Ministry‟s respons e regarding ongoing monitoring 
mechanisms and third -party evaluations mechanism to oversee the execution of the 
Schemes/Projects to ensure that the physical targets as set in the Output -Outcome 
Framework is achieved without time and cost overruns. However,  the Committee observes 
that the Action Taken Reply, as furnished by the Ministry of Ayush, does not sufficiently 
address the Committee‟s specific recommendation in its letter and spirit for want of  
assurance of conducting structured midterm performance e valuations, incorporating cost -
benefit analysis and a comparative assessment of targets achieved against targets set. 
Additionally, no action taken has been reflected in regard to the publication of such 
evaluations in the public domain to promote transpar ency and public confidence. The 
Committee, therefore, reiterates the need for comprehensive mid -term evaluations, 
inclusion of financial efficiency metrics, and proactive disclosure of outcomes to ensure 
timely course correction and enhanced accountability to bring forth the catalytic changes in 
providing Ayurvedic and Homeopathic mode of treatment to all. 
(Para 3.6.2 of the ATR) 
6. The Committee has been apprised by the Ministry that Public Health, being a State 
Subject, the primary responsibility to bridge infrastructure gaps with respect to Ayush 
system in the country, including rural and underserved regions,  lies with the respective 
State/UT Governments.  The Committee finds the response of the Ministry evasive which 
appears that the Ministry is shruggin g of its responsibility. The Committee understands 
that the Ministry has a goal to enhance AYUSH services, particularly, in underserved and 
remote areas, but States/UTs lacks the requisite resources to do so, effectively. The 
Committee, therefore, recommen ds that the Ministry should undertake steps for 
encouraging States/UTs to propose under State Annual Action Plans (SAAP) for 
establishment of integrated Ayush Hospitals in backward regions of the Country 

138 
 
specifically districts with low human development in dex in the State of Orissa, Jharkhand, 
Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan and Bihar (particularly in Koshi/Simanchal Region covering 
Purnia, Kathiar, Saharsa, Madipura, Forbesganj, Araria, Kishanganj). The Committee, 
therefore, desires that the Ministry take a syst ematic, analytical approach in assessing 
parameters and identification of sites in underserved districts for establishing of Ayush 
Medical College and Hospital in a time bound manner to effectively implement its 
cherished goals of placing AYUSH system of h ealthcare in the remotest parts of the 
country, even if it needs handholding the states in creating SAAPs in regard to AYUSH. 
(Para 3.7.2 of the ATR) 
7. The Committee understands that the contribution ratio of Centre and State in a 
Centrally Sponsored Sche me falls under the purview of the Ministry of Finance. The 
Ministry of Ayush may take the matter of modification in existing ratio of 60% and 40% to 
70% and 30% of Centre and State, so that economically weaker States may become part 
and parcel of the Natio nal Ayush Mission (NAM). Since, the implementation of the NAM 
Scheme is mandated under Ministry of Ayush, it is imperative on the part of the Ministry of 
Ayush that no stone is left unturned by the Ministry in convincing a change in the 
contribution ratio. The Committee, strongly recommends the Ministry of Ayush to intiate 
communication with Ministry of Finance for increased allocation and the change in the 
contribution ratio to achieve cherished objective of the scheme. The Committee further 
recommends the  Ministry to explore the possibility of providing financial assistance and 
other forms of incentives so as to bring onboard the States/UTs that are unable to 
contribute their share into NAM scheme. 
(Para 3.8.2 of the ATR) 
8. The Committee commends the Mini stry of AYUSH for its proactive engagement 
with the 16th Finance Commission and the significant strides made by various constituent 
bodies in advancing AYUSH integration into mainstream healthcare. Notable achievements 
include international collaborations such as the MoU with Indonesia on Traditional 
Medicine quality assurance, large -scale research initiatives addressing public health 
concerns like anemia, Academia -industry Research Incubation for Value Up -gradation by 
CCRS and capacity -building efforts thr ough international training programs. These 
initiatives reflect a strong commitment to enhancing AYUSH‟s role in health promotion, 
research, and global outreach . The Committee, however, recommends that the Ministry 
expedite the finalization and implementat ion of a comprehensive mission -mode scheme, 
ensuring timely and adequate fund allocation under the 16th Finance Commission to 
sustain and accelerate AYUSH‟s growth trajectory over the coming five years. Further, the 
Committee observes that some of the inst itutes under AYUSH has just informed “noted” in 
their ATN which is against the Parliamentary etiquettes and dignity. The Committee 
expects all the bodies under AYUSH to be equally and proactively involved in the 
furtherment of the objectives of Ayush systems and the Ministry of Ayush must ensure that 
all autonomous bodies and research councils play its pivotal role in providing exceptional 
ayush service to the country. 
(Para 3.9.2 of the ATR) 

139 
 
9. The Committee is not satisfied with the response of the Minist ry in regard to its 
recommendation for optimizing the strategic reach of the Ayush System. The Committee, 
therefore, reiterates that the Ministry should explore the possibility of setting up of Special 
Export Zones and more Regional Centers to optimize the  strategic reach of the Ayush 
Systems. The Committee also recommends the Ministry to promote medicinal plant 
cultivation and invest in R&D to demonstrate the effectiveness of Ayush Systems to 
potentially attracting more funding and support. In this regard,  NMPB is expected to 
emphasise on R&D on medicinal plants, development of Agro -technologies, post -harvest 
management for expanding the horizon of Ayush System in underserved region as well as 
global market. The Ministry of Ayush must direct - NMPB managemen t to strive in this 
direction. 
(Para 3.10.2 of the ATR) 
10. The Committee appreciates the efforts of the National Medicinal Plants Board 
(NMPB) in supporting farmers and stakeholders through infrastructure, skill development, 
and quality certification. How ever, to fully realize the potential of the medicinal plants 
sector as a catalyst of economic growth and livelihood enhancement, the Committee 
recommends that NMPB further strengthen its initiatives by promoting entrepreneurship 
and innovation. This includ es launching incubation programs, facilitating market access 
through digital platforms, and piloting cluster -based and community -owned models. 
Special focus should be given to tribal and marginalized communities to integrate 
traditional knowledge into form al value chains. Additionally, a robust monitoring and 
evaluation framework should be established to assess the impact of these initiatives, 
ensuring sustainable and inclusive growth of all stakeholders. The Committee firmly 
believes that effective impleme ntation of Central Sector Scheme in Conservation, 
Development and Sustainable Management of Medicinal Plants would go a long way in 
promoting medicinal plant sector. Therefore, NMPB must formulate innovative schemes so 
that farmers engaged in cultivation o f medicinal plants would enhance their livelihood 
system.  
(Para 3.11.2 of the ATR) 
 
11. The Committee is dismayed to observe that the Committee‟s recommendation for a 
single independent drug controller for Ayush hasn‟t been complied with yet. The 
Committee thus reiterates its recommendation for consolidating all AYUSH drug -related 
standard-setting processes under a single authoritative body viz. an independent drug 
controller of AYUSH, in alignment with the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940, and its 
associated rules. To achieve this, the Ministry should establish a streamlined and inclusive 
mechanism that actively involves stakeholders in the development of pharmacopoeial 
standards uniformly, ensuring enhanced safety, quality and efficacy of Ayush medicines 
nationwide. For that sake and also for strengthening the foundation of research and 
uniform standardization in Ayush Sector, PCIM&H & various research councils of Ayush 

140 
 
viz. CCRAS, CCRUM, CCRS and CCRH work together with due co -ordination and 
collaboration.  
 
(Para 3.12.2 of the ATR) 
 
12. The Committee expresses concern that the Action Taken Reply merely states 
"Noted" without providing any assurance or indication of action taken so far regarding the 
proposed extension of the AYURGYAN Scheme beyond 2025 –26. Co nsidering the 
Scheme‟s critical role in promoting education, research, training, and capacity building in 
the Ayush sector, the Committee reiterates its recommendation for extending the scheme 
beyond its current timeline i.e. 31 st March, 2026. The Ministry is advised to initiate a timely 
review of the scheme's impact and utility, and to formulate a proposal for its continuation 
with enhanced scope and adequate budgetary allocation. The Committee would like to 
know whether the Ministry has approached the Min istry of Finance for approval of the 
Scheme‟s extension. A detailed response indicating steps taken in this regard along with the 
response of the Ministry of Finance thereto, if any, may be furnished to the Committee for 
consideration. 
(Para 3.13.2 of the ATR) 
13. The Committee expresses concern that the Action Taken Reply merely contains 
"Noted" in compliance of its recommendation without providing any substantive response 
or assurance regarding the proposed extension of the Ayurswasthya Yojana beyond FY 
2025–26. Considering the vital role played by the scheme, particularly its components that 
include Public Health Initiative (PHI) and Centre of Excellence (CoE) in strengthening Ayush-
based healthcare delivery, promoting research, and supporting integration  with national 
public health priorities, the Committee reiterates its recommendation for the continuation

comprehensive impact assessment and initiate timely steps for extensi on with adequate 
budgetary provisions for the Scheme. The Committee would like to know whether the 
Ministry has approached the Ministry of Finance for approval of the Scheme‟s extension 
and what has been the response thereto. A detailed and time -bound acti on plan may be 
submitted to the Committee for further review. 
(Para 3.14.2 of the ATR) 
 
14. The Committee appreciates the role of a Pharmacopoeia Commission in developing 
and amending Pharmacopoeia and formularies for Ayush system and thus setting 
standards for Ayush drugs ensuring their quality, safety and efficacy and surveillance of 
misleading advertisement. However, the Committee reiterates the creation of a separate 
platform/body to exclusively implement, facilitate and monitor the Pharmcoviligilance o f 
Ayush Drugs. The Committee feels that surveillance of ADRs is crucial for ensuring the 
safety of drugs including those from traditional systems like ASU&H, IPvCs and PPvCs 
which may contribute to these efforts. The Committee recommends the Ministry to 
strengthen Pharmacovigilance systems and promote ADR reporting for protecting the 
public health through NPC -ISM, IPvCs and PPvCs which plays a vital role in ensuring the 

141 
 
safe use of ASU&H drugs. Adequate financial support may be earmarked for research 
components exclusively to evaluate herb drug interaction and further to assess the quality 
and safety aspects of AYUSH drugs. 
(Para 3.15.2 of the ATR) 
15. The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s initiative to create an online portal for 
reporting and tracking misleading or objectionable AYUSH advertisements. However, the 
Committee emphasizes that an online reporting mechanism alone is insufficient to address 
the growing number of violations, which reached 15,873 incidents in 2023 -24 and 2024 -25 
(up to December 2024). The Ministry must clearly identify and empower the authorities 
responsible for investigating and taking timely action against offenders. Moreover, the 
Committee is of the considered view that strict penal provisions should be incorporated into 
the relevant laws or regulations to deter and penalize those making false and misleading 
advertisement/claims, particularly, those asserting quick cures for serious and life -
threatening diseases. The Committee further recommends that the Ministry establish a 
transparent monitoring and reporting framework, through which it regularly updates on 
enforcement actions taken, including the number of investigations, prosecutions, penalties 
imposed, and cases resolved. This will ensure accountability, strengthen deterren ce, and 
enhance public trust in AYUSH healthcare system. 
(Para 3.16.2 of the ATR) 
16. The Committee notes that the national awareness programme, 
“DeshkaPrakritiParikshan,” aims to promote Ayurveda across the country by reaching a 
wide population base, with  over 1.43 crore people covered so far. However, the Committee 
expresses concern that the Action Taken Reply merely states "Noted for Compliance" 
without providing details on concrete steps taken by NCISM to ensure rational utilization 
of funds, acquisition of necessary equipment and technology, and appointment of members 
and regular staff. The Ministry is urged to direct NCISM to formulate and implement a 
comprehensive action plan addressing these critical areas and submit a detailed progress 
report to the Committee at the earliest. 
(Para 3.17.2 of the ATR) 
17. The Committee is dismayed at the response of the Ministry in regard to the 
Committee's recommendation to CCRUM, in taking firm reassessment of its short term and 
long term goals in regard to primary research activities. The Ministry has just expressed 
noted in its ATN which is against the letter and spirit of the recommendation of the 
Committee. The Committee, in this regard, desires that the Ministry must guide CCRUM in 
taking a firm reassessment of its short-term and long -term goals and accordingly chalked 
out plans to address the issues pertaining to experimental Pharmacology of Important 
Unani Drugs, Phytochemical studies, drug standardization, and Pharmacognostic Studies of 
Important Unani Drugs and outcome, thereof would provide treatment of diseases to needy 

142 
 
persons in time at affordable cost. CCRUM must strengthen the Clinical research in the 
priority areas including new drug development and clinical Validation of pharmacopoeial 
drugs.  
(Para 3.18.2 of the ATR) 
18. The Committee is perplexed to observe the evasive and incomplete reply as received 
from the Ministry, in its ATN, on Committee‟s recommendation steps undertaken by AIIA 
during last three years towards its vision. The Committee fails to  understand the

AIIA‟s development and ensure enhanced budgetary allocation to AIIA with the intention 
to bring AIIA at par with ITRA in terms of national importance. 
(Para 3.19.2 of the ATR) 
19. The Committee is anguished to observe that in regard to its recommendation to the 
Ministry of Ayush for ensuring adequate inf rastructure and manpower to the National 
Institute of Sowa-Rigpa (NISR) in the Himalayan region to provide healthcare to the public 
under Sowa -Rigpa system of medicine, the Ministry, in its Action Taken Notes has just

scientific outlook to the knowledge of Sowa -Rigpa System of Medicine, the Committee 
considers that the Sowa -Rigpa system should flourish in the Himalayan Region. The

clinic on wheels etc. T he Committee further recommends the Ministry to guide NISR for

treatment under Sowa-Rigpa system of medicine.    
(Para 3.20.2 of the ATR) 
20. The Committee is not satisfied with the response of various autonomous bodies and 
institutes/councils of the Ministry, in regard to number of posts sanctioned, in -position

143 
 
of the Committee has „Point Noted‟. While other institutes like CCRS, NIS, CCRUM,

assess the vacant post/position in various autonomous and research councils and take 
centralized course of act ion for filling up the vacant position (estimated at more than 2600 
posts) at the accelerated pace taking into consideration the futuristic development plans of 
Ayush Healthcare, Ayush@2047.    
(Para 3.21.2 of the ATR) 
21. The Committee observes that the a ction taken note speaks of union cabinet‟s 
approving of National Ayush Mission (NAM) from 1.04.2021 to 31.03.2026 and under that 
eight Ayush Public Health programmes has been also approved as a new activity i.e. (1) 
National Program for Prevention and Mana gement of Musculoskeletal Disorders, (2) 
Integration of Ayush with National Programme for Prevention and Control of Cancer, 
Diabetes, Cardiovascular Diseases and Stroke (NPCDCS), (3) Supraja, (4) VayoMitra, (5) 
Ayurvidya, (6) Ayush Mobile Medial Unit, (7) Karunya and (8) National Program on Ayush 
for morbidity management and Disability Prevention (MMDP) of Lymphatic Filariasis 
(lymphoedema). Keeping in view the ensuing the third quarter of 2025, just few months 
away from the end date of the Mission, yet the  Ministry is reiterating the old trope of 
initiation of 8 Ayush Public Health Programmes appearing oblivious of the upcoming 
closure of the Mission, the Committee recommends the Ministry to approach the Ministry 
of Finance for seeking extension of NAM for another 5 years (01.04.2026 to 31.03.2031). 
The Ministry of Ayush must ensure that the States/UTs are encouraged to come under the 
umbrella of NAM. The Committee feels that the given 8 schemes represent a solid 
foundation addressing several key public heal th areas through  AYUSH systems  but the 
country may benefit from expansion or supplementary schemes to comprehensively cover 
infectious diseases, mental health, and broader preventive health strategies. The Ministry of 
Ayush is suggested to expand the scope  of the Mission to cover the aforementioned areas of 
healthcare.  
(Para 3.22.2 of the ATR) 
22. The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Ministry and State Government of 
Sikkim for setting up of Ayush Directorate for promotion of traditional medicines i n their 
State. The Committee hopes that Ministry must play a vital role in supporting North 
Eastern States in establishing dedicated AYUSH Directorates in all NER States. The 
Committee urges the Ministry to encourage the States and provide all the necessar y 
handholding required by the State Governments for converting this vision a ground reality. 
This will pave the way to harness the potential of Ayush Systems, promoting holistic 
healthcare and well-being in the region. 
(Para 3.23.2 of the ATR) 

144 
 
23. The Committee is of the considered view that the effective implementation of NAM 
scheme in NER is concomitant to establishement of Project Management Unit (PMU) with 
sufficient manpower and dedicated Ayush Directorate to ensure adequate monitoring and 
positioning of Ayush related issues. The Committee notes that although Manipur, Assam, 
Nagaland, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram have established PMU as per provision of NAM 
guidelines. While Arunachal Pradesh and Sikkim have although established PMU but 
without having full strength of manpower. This is really a sorry state of affairs for effective 
implementation of NAM Scheme in these two States. The Committee, therefore, strongly 
recommends the Ministry of Ayush to effectively persuade North -Eastern States for 
dedicated Ayush Directorate and PMU in Arunachal Pradesh and Sikkim must be 
equipped with adequate manpower. 
(Para 3.24.2 of the ATR) 
24. The Committee expresses its concern over the delay in completion of infrastructure 
projects viz. 16 integrated Ayush Hospitals a pproved during 2014 -15 to 2021 -22 under 
NAM Scheme through SAAP due to slow progress in completing construction work. 
However, the Committee notes that 10 integrated Ayush Hospitals are functional, 03 
Hospitals construction completed but not functional and  remaining 03 are still under 
construction. The Committee, therefore, desires that three hospitals construction that have 
been completed but not functional must be made functional without further delay and 
accelerated progress must be made for completing t he remaining three infrastructure 
projects so that the same is also made functional without much procrastination.  
(Para 3.25.2 of the ATR) 
25. The Committee appreciates the work being carried out by various autonomous 
bodies and research councils under AY USH in response to the concerns raised by the 
Members of the Committee. The Committee, however, recommends that the Ministry of 
AYUSH undertake comprehensive public awareness campaigns to elucidate the authentic 
benefits of Ayurveda, while simultaneously i mplementing stringent measures to address 
quackery and misleading advertisements, particularly those purporting rapid cures for 
critical ailments such as cancer and chronic kidney disease. The introduction of robust 
penal provisions is imperative to deter deceptive claims that compromise the credibility of 
AYUSH systems. Furthermore, the Committee emphasizes the need to extend Yoga 
awareness initiatives beyond the observance of World Yoga Day, ensuring sustained 
outreach at rural and block levels throughout  the year. Priority should also be accorded to 
the establishment of AYUSH institutes and Yoga Ashrams in underdeveloped states 
including Bihar, Jharkhand, and Uttar Pradesh. Adequate budgetary provisions must be 
allocated for clinical trials and scientific  validation centers to bolster evidence -based 
research and promote medical tourism in regions abundant in medicinal plants, such as 
Kerala, Karnataka, and the Northeast. 
(Para 3.26.2 of the ATR) 

145 
 
26. The Committee reiterates that the M/o Ayush should undert ake concrete steps in 
compliance of the suggestions/issues raised by Members of the Committee in Para 4.1 in the 
165th Report of the Committee.  
(Para 3.27.2 of the ATR) 
27. The Committee is deeply concerned that only Rs. 475.00 crore worth of Utilization 
Certificates (UCs) have been liquidated in 2024 -25, which is grossly insufficient given the 
backlog of over 1,500 pending UCs under the National AYUSH Mission (NAM). Given the 
critical role UCs play in ensuring transparency, accountability, and effective s cheme 
implementation, the mounting issue of pending liquidation of UCs should be a matter of 
concern for the Ministry. This shortcoming hampers timely monitoring and subsequent 
fund releases, adversely affecting project outcomes. The Committee, therefore, urges the 
Ministry to intensify efforts to expedite the clearance of pending UCs, particularly under 
the National AYUSH Mission, to meet prescribed timelines. The Committee also 
recommends that the Ministry set a target to limit pending UCs below 10% of th e total UCs 
at any given time and eventually it shall move towards nil pendency.   The Ministry should 
strengthen coordination with States and UTs, incentivize timely submissions, and promote 
optimal use of the NAM portal to ensure prompt and complete liqu idation of UCs, thereby 
safeguarding the integrity and success of AYUSH schemes. The Committee may be 
apprised of the updated status of UCs for the year 2023-24 and 2024-25. 
(Para 3.28.2 of the ATR) 
28. The Committee appreciates the role of Ministry of Ayu sh in pushing e -AUSHADHI 
Portal more user friendly and robust for online licensing of Ayurveda, Siddha, Unani and 
Homeopathy Drugs, however, the action so take would ensure transparency in issuance of 
licencing process. The Committee‟s main emphasis is on evolving a standard licensing 
process based on uniform parameters across all states so that drug formulations do not 
vary from State to State, thereby enhancing the quality, safety and efficacy of Ayush 
Medicine. The Committee, therefore, recommends the Mi nistry to strive for a nationally 
recognized quality standard that will entail consumer confidence and ultimately foster 
weather acceptance and integration of Ayush medicine in mainstream healthcare.  
(Para 3.29.2 of the ATR) 
 
29. The Committee wanted to d rive home the fact that a simplified procedure for 
seeking uniform and standard license would ensure ease of doing business for Ayush 
manufacturers, thus having an eye on achieving the core objective of „Atmanirbhar Bharat‟ 
and „Make in India‟ Mission. The  Committee recommends the Ministry to facilitate the 
manufactures willing to seek license for production of Ayush products by setting up Ayush 

146 
 
industry in the country so that manufacturers of Ayush products can promote Ayush 
products in domestic as well as the international markets.   
(Para 3.30.2 of the ATR) 
30. The Committee is in agreement with the submission of CCRS that an effective 
CTMS in Ayush will be of importance for integrating traditional knowledge to current 
scientific validation, increasing re search outcomes and making Ayush -based interventions 
universally acceptable and scalable. A CTMS assures systematic documentation, protocol 
compliance, and data integrity in preparation for regulatory clearances. A CTMS ensures 
real-time data capture, vali dation and safe storage while decreasing human error and bias. 
It aids in automating critical trial processes such as patient recruiting, randomization, 
monitoring, and reporting in a successful manner. Since, a well -integrated CTMS will allow 
multicenter trials, facilitating global acceptance of AYUSH -based interventions and 
positioning India as a leader in integrative medicine research, the Committee recommends 
the Ministry of Ayush to encourage all its autonomous bodies and research councils to 
bring-forth clinical trials and related activities onto single platform i.e. Clinical Trial 
Management System (CTMS) which would significantly improve documentation efficiency 
and mandatory compliance in Ayush clinical research.     
(Para 3.31.2 of the ATR) 
31. The Committee notes the Ministry‟s response regarding updates by the 
Homoeopathy Education Board on incorporating interdisciplinary knowledge, research 
methodology, and skill enhancement in the syllabus. However, the Action Taken lacks 
clarity on how soft ski lls such as communication, ethical responsibility, discipline, and 
professionalism are explicitly integrated across all AYUSH disciplines. Further, the 
Committee expresses its concern that a comprehensive response hasn‟t been submitted 
covering all the aut onomous bodies/institute and research councils under AYUSH. The 
Committee, therefore, may be apprised about the actions that have been taken by all the 
bodies concerned with that matter under AYUSH.  Additionally, the recommendation to 
modernize the curric ulum through technology integration and creation of a technology -
enabled workforce remains unaddressed. The Committee urges the Ministry to ensure a 
comprehensive revision of curricula across all AYUSH streams that systematically 
incorporates soft skills t raining and modern technological competencies. A detailed 
implementation plan with timelines and measurable outcomes should be submitted for the 
Committee‟s review. 
(Para 3.32.2 of the ATR) 
32. The Committee expresses concern that the Ministry has simply m entioned “Noted” 
in its ATN in compliance of the Committee‟s recommendation on education in integrated 
healthcare. The Committee expects the Ministry to provide comprehensive details of the 
Action Taken on the given recommendation that internship in Ayush MBBS curriculum be 

147 
 
enhanced to atleast 15 days. The Committee is of firm view that such a step would ensure a 
holistic knowledge sharing between conventional and alternative system of medicine. 
Furthermore, the Ministry has not addressed the recommendation  that one -year nursing 
course started within Ayush be adopted on a wider scale. The Committee desires to be 
apprised of the steps taken by the Ministry so far to propagate the nursing course across 
India as per healthcare requirements.   
(Para 3.33.2 of the ATR) 
33. The Committee expresses its dissatisfaction with the half hearted approach of the 
Ministry to opening up more Ayush Centre in AIIMS and other Government Institutes of 
the country. The Committee thus reiterates that Department of Ayush be setup i n all 
AIIMS across the country in a time bound manner with adequate manpower and resources 
as necessary for the smooth running of the department for better service of the public. The 
Committee further recommends that a collaborative effort between the Mini stry of Ayush 
and Union Ministry of Health to establish a pluralistic healthcare system to enhance 
healthcare delivery, promote well-being and preserve traditional knowledge of medicine.  
(Para 3.34.2 of the ATR) 
 
34. The Committee reiterates that a separa te centre of Ayush may also be set up in 
Parliament Complex for treatment of MPs, Ex -MPs and Employees and Ex -Employees of 
Sansad that would not only ensure treatment of Members of Parliament through Ayush 
System but would ensure the popularization/awareness of Ayush to each constituency as the 
Member of Parliament would prove a harbinger to awareness of common masses across the 
county.  
(Para 3.34.3 of the ATR) 
35. The Committee expresses concern that the recommendation on expanding the scope 
of treatment of Sickle Cell Anemia in the Tribal areas and in northeast regions with 
Collaboration with M/o Tribal Affairs has been responded with a single clause “Noted for 
compliance”. The Committee understands that India has a significant young population 
affected b y Sickle Cell Disease (SCD). The global share of India in SCD is also high. 
Therefore, it is imperative that the Ministry takes all the steps necessary to effectively treat 
such diseases. The Committee wants to know the exact action taken by the Ministry t o 
implement the Committee‟s recommendation on ground level with details of plans. The 
Committee also wants to know the positive impacts that have been created via Eklavya 
Residential Model Schools in quantitave terms. 
(Para 3.35.2 of the ATR) 
 

148 
 
36. The Comm ittee appreciates the action taken by various bodies under AYUSH in 
implementation of its recommendation to bring into existence a cooperative and 
collaborative synthesis between allopathy and Ayush system of medicine. However, the 
Committee expresses conc ern that still some of the institutes/bodies under Ayush has in 
their action taken just mentioned “noted” or just expressed agreement with Committee‟s 
recommendation or speaking in future tense without mentioning any steps that has actually 
been taken on t heir end to implement the Committee‟s recommendation at present. The 
Committee is of the considered view, keeping in consideration the cherished goals of the 
Ministry to have integrated healthcare wherein Ayush and Allopathy go hand in hand to 
put Indian Healthcare at a commanding position in the world.  
(Para 3.36.2 of the ATR) 
 
37. The Committee is concerned that the Ministry of Ayush has only stated that  it is 
promoting Yoga and Naturopathy nationwide  through various initiatives and programmes 
across the country. However, the response reveals significant shortcomings as the Ministry 
of Ayush hasn‟t mentioned the details of such initiatives and programmes specifically in 
regard to naturopathy. The establishment of National Ayush Institutes in underserved  
states such as Bihar, Odisha, Uttar Pradesh, Jharkhand and the North Eastern Region has 
been deferred to State Governments without a concrete central plan or support mechanism, 
risking continued regional disparities. Similarly, the integration of Yoga cla sses and 
instructors in primary schools is delegated to the Ministry of Education and State 
Governments, with no clear inter -ministerial coordination outlined. To address these gaps, 
the Committee recommends that the Ministry of AYUSH to actively collabora te with 
Department of School Education and relevant stakeholders in various states to facilitate 
and support the establishment of institutes and the mandatory inclusion of Yoga education 
in schools, particularly in underserved areas. 
(Para 3.37.2 of the ATR) 
38. Keeping in view the fact of unequal distribution of Ayush healthcare in the country, 
the Committee is of the considered view that the Ministry of Ayush must ensure equitable 
development of Ayush healthcare and eco -system across the geographical area  of the 
country. In this regard, the Committee appreciates the role of the Ministry of Ayush for 
granting approval of setting up of 167 integrated Ayush Hospitals and 1055 Ayush Grams 
as per the proposals received from States/UTs Government through SAAPs u nder NAM. 
The Committee has been given to understand that under NAM there is provision of 
financial assistance for setting up of integraed Ayush Hospitals, therefore, the Committee 
recommends the Ministry to expedite the process ensuring priority to areas where the 
Ayush healthcare has not progressed to the expected level. The Committee further 
recommends that the Ministry should encourage States/UTs with lesser economic 
development viz. Jharkhand, Odisha, Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan, Uttar Pradesh and 
Bihar (especially Seemanchal/koshi region) to seek proposals through SAAPs for setting up 
of integrated Ayush hospitals and Ayush Grams. The Committee further recommends the 

149 
 
Ministry to implement the proposal for 50 bedded Ayush hospitals in every district and 1 00 
bedded hospitals in all tier-2 cities.  
(Para 3.38.2 of the ATR) 
39. The Committee notes that only some  of the bodies under AYUSH has provideds its 
Action Taken Notes in compliance with the Committee‟s recommendation. The Committee 
therefore, recommends the Ministry that a comprehensive Action Taken be provided at the 
earliest to assess the on -ground situation in respect of Committee‟s recommendation. The 
Committee appreaciates the larger vision of Ayush@2047 however, to effectively address 
the Committe e's recommendations, the Ministry of AYUSH should develop a 
comprehensive short -term action plan, clearly delineating measurable targets and 
timelines, with provisions for mid -term evaluations to assess progress. Therefore, the 
Committee recommends that th e Annual Action Plans of each organ of AYUSH be strictly 
monitored and outcome thereof be vetted properly ensuring that the year plans are 
executed fully. The Budgetary allocations must be aligned with both long -term objectives 
and immediate priorities, en suring optimal fund utilization through a robust and adaptive 
monitoring mechanism. Immediate actions should include the initiation of pilot projects 
and awareness campaigns at the regional level, aiming for measurable outcomes within 6-12 
months. To mitigate deviations, the Ministry should establish real -time monitoring systems 
and mechanisms for timely course correction. Furthermore, research initiatives should be 
expedited, particularly focusing on the clinical validation of AYUSH practices. Enhanced 
inter-departmental coordination is crucial for integrating AYUSH into broader national 
health strategies and fostering international collaboration. 
(Para 3.39.2 of the ATR) 
40. The Committee acknowledges and appreciates the commendable efforts of AIIA and 
CCRAS in advancing AYUSH through clinical services, international collaborations, 
research initiatives, capacity building, and public health programs. However, considering 
the Ministry of AYUSH encompasses multiple institutions and autonomous bodies, the 
Committee seeks clarification on whether the onus of these initiatives will rest exclusively 
with AIIA and CCRAS. Additionally, while the Ministry has articulated an ambition to 
realize a market capitalization of USD 200 billion for AYUSH within next five year s, the 
Committee urges the submission of a detailed, year -wise strategic plan outlining 
implementation frameworks, institutional responsibilities, and resource allocation. A 
comprehensive response with details of schemes and plans for growth and developmen t of 
Ayush activities and the Ministry should project a much more enhanced budgetary 
allocation. 
(Para 3.40.2 of the ATR) 
 

150 
 
41. The Committee appreciates the bilateral agreement of the Ministry of Ayush with 
the Ministry of Commerce and Industry to include Ayush products. The Committee 
however wants to know the steps taken by the Ministry of Ayush to establish Ayush specific 
healthcare centres in PHCs/CHCs at district level in all the states across India. The 
Committee appreciates the larger goal of the Mini stry of Ayush to have 30 lakh ayush 
professionals in the country by 2047. However, to effectively realize the goal it is imperative 
on the part of the Ministry to split it into short term goals. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry of Ayu sh come up with comprehensive yearly plans of 
producing a minimum number of say 1 lakh Ayush Professionals each year. In extension, 
the Ministry must chalk out the plan for absorption of these professionals in appropriate 
jobs. Hence job creation is a must  in Ayush Healthcare to achieve the cherished goals of 
Ayush@2047. Further, as suggested by NISR, the Committee endorses the opinion that to 
provide health to all it is suggested to strengthen the local health traditions and train the 
traditional healer in primary health care in remote areas. The Committee recommends the 
Ministry of Ayush to find out the traditional health practitioners in remotest areas of the 
country and archive their knowledge in traditional healing which can be utilized for a 
larger population of this country and beyond.  
(Para 3.41.2 of the ATR) 
42. The Committee observes that the Ministry of AYUSH has undertaken commendable 
initiatives to promote AYUSH systems globally, notably through the International 
Cooperation Scheme, academic col laborations, and the implementation of accredited 
training programs under the Ayurveda Training Accreditation Board (ATAB) and Yoga 
Certification Board (YCB). The contributions of institutions such as AIIA, CCRAS and 
ITRA in disseminating and operationaliz ing these programs are noteworthy. However, the 
action taken does not fully align with the Committee‟s recommendation in letter and spirit 
to significantly enhance global participation in Yoga and Ayurveda training. However, the 
current measures appear to remain largely domestic in scope and lack a comprehensive 
international outreach framework. The Committee strongly feels that there is an immense 
potential in Ayush systems on the global stage and it must be promoted on a global level 
based on unique stren gths of Ayurveda, Yoga and Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Rigpa -
Sowa and Homeopathy. It is recommended that the Ministry develop a cohesive global 
strategy encompassing international partnerships, welcoming international students to get 
Yoga Certification in India under aforementioned schemes. The Ministry of Ayush should 
strive to make the ayurveda institutes of highest international repute, that all over the 
world people would want to get education in traditional system of medicine especially in 
Ayurveda, Yoga & Naturopathy to begin with.  
(Para 3.42.2 of the ATR)
